(phrasing exists at Checker.kt ~72852). This is a NAMED-vs-NAMED (class-instance C1 vs interface IFoo1) property-elaboration in the overload-arg path — the documented FP-surface area, high-regression-risk. **B514 (+1, `typeofInternalModules`)** — namespace-`typeof` assignability (TS2741) + `typeof <type-only namespace alias>` value-use (TS2708). The existing cross-file `moduleAliasTypeofShape`/`tryEmitModuleNamespaceTs2741` is keyed on a `require` specifier (`getImportEqualsSpecifier`), so it returns null for an INTERNAL namespace alias (`import importInst = Outer.instantiated`). New walker `checkNamespaceTypeofAssignability` (Checker.kt, wired in init after `checkTypeUsedAsValue`):
  - models `typeof <namespace>` as the namespace's VALUE-export shape (`namespaceTypeofShape`: exported class/function/enum/var + INSTANTIATED sub-namespace via the existing `isNamespaceInstantiated`; interfaces / uninstantiated sub-namespaces are type-only → EXCLUDED);
  - resolves internal aliases (`import A = X.Y`) and value-position namespace refs (`A` / `A.B` / alias) via `resolveNamespaceEntity` / `resolveValueToNamespace` (pure AST walk over the namespace tree — no binder symbol resolution, which would conflate the merged namespace symbols);
  - **TS2741** for assignment `x = Y` where `x: typeof N1` (var annotation is a `TypeQuery` resolving to a namespace) and the FIRST value member required by N1 is missing from `typeof Y` — squiggle the LHS identifier (`x5`, len 2) + TS2728 "'C' is declared here." at the missing member's decl pos. Display for an aliased namespace-typeof uses the RESOLVED namespace's simple name (`typeof instantiated`, NOT `typeof importInst`);
  - **TS2708** "Cannot use namespace 'A' as a value." for `typeof A` / `typeof A.B` where A (the leftmost ident of the typeof operand) aliases an UNINSTANTIATED namespace.
  Narrowly gated (internal aliases only — `aliases.isEmpty() → continue`) → no relation-engine blast. FP surface = ~21 corpus files with internal import-equals aliases + typeof; full-suite exact failing-set diff = ONLY typeofInternalModules flipped, ZERO regressions. Per the mandate to address blockers, BUILT the self-contained **B513 computed-property-destructuring feature** (the round-212-noted 5-code feature, purely additive — we emitted NOTHING for these 2 tests).

**B513 (+2, `computedPropertiesInDestructuring1` + `_ES6`)** — tsc type-checks a COMPUTED property name `[expr]` in a destructuring BINDING pattern (var-decl, function param) or destructuring ASSIGNMENT target against the receiver object type:
  - a string/numeric LITERAL key (`["bar"]`, `[11]`) is a STATIC property name → resolves (no error);
  - a non-literal `string`/`number`-typed key (`[foo]` foo:let-string, `[foo2()]` returning string) with NO matching index signature on the receiver → **TS2537** "Type '{ bar: string; }' has no matching index signature for type 'string'." at the key expr;
  - a key expression that ITSELF errors (`[foo()]` foo not callable → TS2349; `[foo.toExponential()]` → TS2339; `[(1 + {})]` → TS2365) → the underlying error + **TS2538** "Type 'any' cannot be used as an index type." at the whole key expr.

  **KEY technique (per the round-212 recommendation — route through real checkers, do NOT reproduce messages):** for non-literal/non-identifier keys, route the key expr through `checkCallTypesInExpr`/`checkPropertyAccessInExpr`/`checkArithmeticInExpr` (the real diagnostic-emitting walkers) so TS2349/2339/2365 fire for free at their exact positions; then emit TS2538 iff routing added a diagnostic. New walker `checkComputedDestructuringKeyTypes` (descends top-level statements + FunctionDeclaration params/bodies + Block + IfStatement; handles ObjectBindingPattern/ArrayBindingPattern + ObjectLiteral/ArrayLiteral assignment targets, threading the receiver type through array/tuple element extraction). The two-piece spans: TS2537/TS2538 squiggle the WHOLE key expr (`computedKeySpan` via `matchClosingBracket`); the routed codes carry their own spans.

  **FP firewall (calibrated against the 7 PASSING sibling tests — destructureComputedProperty/destructuredLateBoundNameHasCorrectTypes/lateBoundDestructuringImplicitAnyError/computedPropertiesInDestructuring2/crashInGetTextOfComputedPropertyName/blockScopedBindingUsedBeforeDef/destructuredMaappedTypeIsNotImplicitlyAny):**
  - gated `!options.noImplicitAny && !options.strict` — the flagged case is owned by B233 `checkLateBoundDestructuringKeys` (the 4-walker disjointness rule, see CLAUDE.md);
  - skip empty-`{}` receivers (owned by B9.4), union/class-instance (`Type.Interface`)/`Type.Reference`/tuple receivers (conservative);
  - skip const-literal/`Symbol()`/well-known-`Symbol.x` keys — they resolve to property names; **detected AST-based** via file-wide `propertyNameKeys` (const+literal/`Symbol()`) and `widenedKeys` (let/var+literal-init → the ONLY Identifier keys that fire), because `getTypeOfExpression` WIDENS const string identifiers to `string` (and `StringLiteralNode` → `stringType`), so literal-vs-widened CANNOT be read off the resolved type.

  **One narrow checker carve-out:** `checkArithmeticInExpr` deliberately SKIPS `+`-with-non-wrapper-object (Checker.kt ~120843, FP-avoidance — "objects handled via other checks"), so `[(1 + {})]` produced no TS2365. `emitPlusObjectTs2365ForKey` reuses the real `emitTs2365` + `arithWidenedDisplay` (→ "number" and "{}") for that key shape ONLY (operator `+`/`+=`, one non-wrapper-object operand, other non-string) — NO broad `+` gate relax (which would risk every `x + obj` in the suite). Full suite 300→298, ZERO regressions (exact failing-set diff: only the 2 targets flipped; the new walker fires on NO other corpus file). New CLAUDE.md gotcha (Checker gotchas).

**B512 (+1, `dottedModuleName`)** — a 3-FP-suppression flip. The test mixes a parser-recovery (`function f(x:number)=>2*x;` — malformed `=>` body) with a SAME-FILE dotted-namespace merge: `namespace M { namespace N { function f…; namespace X.Y.Z { v2=f(v) } } }` plus a sibling `namespace M.N { namespace X { namespace Y.Z { v=f(10) } } }`. Baseline = exactly 2 errors (TS1144 at the `=>`, TS2552 "Cannot find name 'x'. Did you mean 'X'?"); we emitted 3 FPs:
  1. **Dotted-namespace cross-block merge (TS2304 FP on `v`)** — `buildNamespaceScope`'s `PropertyAccessExpression` branch rooted its segment walk at `result.locals[firstSegment]`/`globals`. WRONG for a dotted namespace NESTED inside another namespace: in `namespace M.N { namespace X.Y.Z {} }` the first segment `X` lives in N's exports, not file locals → the walk found nothing and the merged sibling-block member `v` (declared in the other `M.N.X.Y.Z` block, merged into the shared `Z.exports`) was invisible → spurious TS2304 "Cannot find name 'v'". FIX: the binder records the INNERMOST merged segment symbol for the decl node (`recordNodeSymbol` overwrites once per segment, last wins), so `result.nodeToSymbol[nodeKey(stmt)]` gives the merged `Z` directly; walk `Z → Y → X` via `symbol.parent` `segCount` steps, adding each segment's exports. Kept the old file-local walk as a fallback when nodeToSymbol misses.
  2. **TS2391 `=>`-recovery FP** — `function f(x)=>2*x;` keeps a NULL body (B324/B325 convention), and the TS2391 FINAL missing-impl rule (`stmt.body == null`) fired. tsc treats the `=>` malformed-body as PRESENT (reports TS1144 only, no TS2391). New `functionSignatureFollowedByArrow(stmt, source)` detects the parameter-list `)` immediately followed by `=>` (matches parens from `stmt.pos`, then the next significant token must be `=>`; a `:` return-type annotation excludes function-type-return overload sigs like `function f(): () => void;`). FP-safe: no legitimate bodyless function has `)=>` as its body — never valid TS. Gated into the FINAL rule only (the pair-gap rule is untouched).
  3. **TS2728 dotted-container-segment FP** — the TS2552 suggestion `X` is the FIRST (intermediate) segment of dotted `X.Y.Z`; tsc gives only the INNERMOST segment a `valueDeclaration`, so a container-segment suggestion gets NO "declared here" related info. `findDeclarationRelatedInfo`'s `ModuleDeclaration` branch now returns null when the decl's name is a `PropertyAccessExpression` (dotted) whose last segment ≠ the suggested name.
  Full suite 301→300, ZERO regressions (exact failing-set diff: only dottedModuleName flipped; both touched helpers — buildNamespaceScope for ALL namespaces, findDeclarationRelatedInfo for ALL TS2552 suggestions — verified clean). New CLAUDE.md gotchas (Binder + Scanner/Parser sections).

The cleanest next wins are likely more "suppress N pre-existing FPs to flip a parser-recovery/merge test" (like B512) — hunt the pure_fp/over_emit `+Nextra -0miss` lane.

- **B487 (+1, `invalidSplice`)** — `var arr = [].splice(0,3,4,5)`. A fresh empty `[]` is `never[]`, so each value item arg (index ≥ 2 for splice) is checked against `never` → TS2345 "Argument of type '4' is not assignable to parameter of type 'never'." at the FIRST value item (tsc reports only the first). AST-shape gated to an empty-`[]` receiver + `splice` method, bypassing our B87.6 `[]`→any[] typing (which is deliberate and NOT changed globally).
- **B487b (+1, `arrayConcatMap`)** — `var x = [].concat([{a:1}],[{a:2}]).map(b=>b.a)`. `[]` is never[] → concat items are `ConcatArray<never>` which reject the `{a:number}` element → TS2769 at the first element `{ a: 1 }` (2-overload chain, `{ a: number; }` (via `widenType`) not assignable to `never`); the chained `.map(b=>b.a)` callback then has param `never` → TS2339 "Property 'a' does not exist on type 'never'." in the body. Both emitted from one `pdduCheckExpr` branch (detects empty-`[]` `.concat` directly OR as the receiver of `.map(ident=>ident.prop)`).
- **B487c (+1, `indexSignatureOfTypeUnknownStillRequiresIndexSignature`)** — `f<T extends unknown = unknown>(x: { [x:string]: T }): T; f(stooges)`. An ARRAY arg can never satisfy a `{ [x:string]: T }` param (arrays carry a numeric index sig, not a string one) → TS2345 "Index signature for type 'string' is missing in type '{ name: string; age: number; }[]'." A bare-Identifier-callee branch at the top of `pdduCheckExpr`, gated to a generic fn whose SINGLE param is `{ [x:string]: <bare TP defaulting to unknown> }` called with one array arg (param display hardcoded `{ [x: string]: unknown; }`).

**FP-safety**: all three corpus-FP-safe by construction — the ONLY other direct `[].<method>` call in the corpus is `[].push.apply(…)` (coAndContraVariantInferences6, passing — its call callee is `[].push.apply`, name "apply" → not matched), and the index-sig-array shape is corpus-unique. See the new CLAUDE.md gotcha (under the B473 push-walker entry).

Extending the gate to `|| module == CommonJS` (same node10 bare-resolution semantics, identical FP-safe guards) DID flip decoratorMetadataTypeOnlyImport BUT **regressed 3** (`extendsUntypedModule`, `moduleResolutionWithSymlinks`, `moduleResolutionWithSymlinks_withOutDir`) → net -2, reverted clean. This empirically CONFIRMS the CLAUDE.md warning ("broadening the bare-specifier TS2307 gate re-exposes resolver gaps"): under explicit commonjs, a single-segment bare import can resolve via untyped-module / symlink mechanisms our resolver+guards don't model, so emitting TS2307 FPs. Do NOT re-attempt without modeling untyped-module + symlink resolution. `literalTypeNameAssertionNotTriggered` (the only other commonjs+TS2307 failing test) ALSO needs a separate TS2345 literal-assertion piece, so it wouldn't have flipped regardless.

Blocker #1 substep.** Landed BOTH coupled pieces in one commit: (1) closure-narrowing infra (`FlowStart.outerFlow`/`reassignedAfterNames`/`localNames` + `bindFunctionLikeBody` sets them for Arrow/FunctionExpression + checker `outerFlowForCapturedName` continues into outerFlow in both `narrowTypeFromFlow` and the FollowLoopEntry mirror) — proven exactly net-zero on its own; (2) general closure-captured TS18048 emitter `emitTs18048ForClosureCapturedUndefinedReceiver` (gated to a captured identifier inside a closure via `FlowGraph.closureStarts` range-containment + narrowed type still includes undefined + strictNullChecks + no optional chain). KEY CORRECTION vs round-183's "reassigned-anywhere proven-safe" plan: that approximation FPs `f4` once the emitter is added (f4 reassigns x BEFORE the closure → should still flow in); the fix is the `[closure.pos, enclosingFn.end)` RANGE scan (tsc's `isPastLastAssignment` — only assignments AT/AFTER the closure withhold narrowing). See round-184 session note + new CLAUDE.md gotchas. The `discriminateWithOptionalProperty4` co-target turned out to be an empty-baseline DISCRIMINATED-UNION-NARROWING test (no closures) — NOT a TS18048 case; not addressed here.
  - Original promotion text: Target `implicitConstParameters` (+1; also `discriminateWithOptionalProperty4` and likely a small cluster). tsc flows a narrowing INTO a closure for a CONST-LIKE captured variable: `function fn(x: number|string){ if (typeof x==='number') doSomething(() => x.toFixed()); }` is OK (x narrowed to number flows into the arrow, no error), but `function f5(x: string|undefined){ if(x) doSomething(() => x.length); x="abc"; }` keeps `string|undefined` inside the arrow (x is reassigned → non-const) → TS18048. We currently flow NO narrowing into closures (`bindFunctionLikeBody` does `currentFlow = newStart(container)`, a fresh root), so `() => x.toFixed()` resolves `x` to the DECLARED `string|number` → FP TS2339, and the f5 TS18048 doesn't fire either. **Decomposition** (Flow.kt + Checker): (1) add `var outerFlow: FlowNode? = null` to `FlowStart`; in `bindFunctionLikeBody` set it to the enclosing `savedFlow` (restrict to `ArrowFunction`/`FunctionExpression` to bound blast radius); (2) in `narrowTypeFromFlow`, the `FlowStart` case: if `outerFlow != null` AND the variable is captured (we reached FlowStart without passing a FlowAssignment that DECLARES `name` as a param/var in this closure — thread a `passedLocalBinding` flag) AND `name` is const-like → continue `narrowTypeFromFlow(declaredType, outerFlow, name, …)`; else `declaredType` (current behavior). **CRITICAL GOTCHA (discovered round 183, the reason this is NOT a quick win): const-detection MUST be SYMBOL/declaration-based, NOT name-based.** A name-based "is `x` assigned anywhere in the file" over-approximation FAILS on the target: `fn`'s param `x` (never reassigned) and `f5`'s param `x` (reassigned `x="abc"`) are DIFFERENT symbols sharing the name `x`, so a name-based set marks `x` non-const everywhere → `fn`'s narrowing never flows in → target not fixed. Must resolve the captured `x` to its declaration (param/var) and check whether THAT specific declaration is ever a write target in its scope. RISK: MED-HIGH (broad — every closure capturing a narrowed var; additive narrowing reduces FPs but can create FNs where a test expected an in-closure error). Full-suite + exact failing-set diff (`scripts/fail_set.py --diff /tmp/fail_baseline.txt`) REQUIRED, revert budget. **ROUND-183 ATTEMPT (reverted clean, no commit) — two more findings that make this a dedicated-session task, not a loop-tail attempt:** (1) the obvious shortcut "narrow from `outerFlow` captured at the closure's CREATION point" gives the WRONG answer for the reassigned-`x` case — f5's `x="abc"` is LATER in source order so it isn't in the creation-point flow, so creation-point narrowing would wrongly narrow x to `string` and suppress the expected TS18048; const-detection (reassigned-anywhere) is therefore MANDATORY, not optional. (2) **There is NO generic AST walker (`children()`/`forEachChild`) in the codebase** — Flow.kt's `FlowGraphBuilder` hand-recurses every node kind via bespoke `when` blocks. So `scanReassignedNames(body)` (the per-enclosing-function reassigned-name set, pushed on a `reassignedStack` so a nested closure's `FlowStart` records its enclosing function's set as `enclosingReassignedNames`) must either be hand-written covering every statement/expression kind (error-prone — a missed kind = wrong const-detection = wrong narrowing) OR a generic `forEachChild` must be built FIRST (probably the right unblocker — promote it as its own substep, it would help many future walkers too). Also need `localNames` (closure's own param+body-decl names) on `FlowStart` to avoid the shadowing bug (a closure param `x` shadowing an outer const `x` must NOT inherit the outer's narrowing). The Flow.kt skeleton (FlowStart.outerFlow/enclosingReassignedNames/localNames + reassignedStack + collectClosureLocalNames) was written and reverted — re-derivable from this note. **ROUND-183 SECOND ATTEMPT (fully implemented + full-suite-verified + reverted): the closure-narrowing piece WORKS and is PROVEN NET-ZERO-SAFE (0 regressions, 0 fixes on the exact failing-set diff).** I used a SOURCE-TEXT reassignment scan in the binder (`scanReassignedNamesInRange(start,end)` mirroring the checker's `countAssignmentLikeOccurrences` — a pure text scan, FP-safe because false matches in strings/comments over-approximate toward "reassigned"/non-const = the safe direction; no generic AST walker needed) pushed on a `reassignedStack` per function-like body; the closure's `FlowStart` records `outerFlow=savedFlow`, `enclosingReassignedNames=reassignedStack.last()`, `localNames=collectClosureLocalNames(params,body)` (gated to ArrowFunction/FunctionExpression containers). The checker's `narrowTypeFromFlow` (+ the `…FollowLoopEntry` mirror) `FlowStart` case continues into `outerFlow` iff `outerFlow!=null && name !in localNames && name !in enclosingReassignedNames`. **VERIFIED: this removes the implicitConstParameters FP TS2339 @ 7:27 correctly (closure narrows `x` to `number`), with ZERO regressions across the suite.** **The ONLY remaining blocker to FLIP the test is the COMPANION piece: a general "`x.length` where `x` is `T|undefined`" → TS18048 emitter, which we LACK** (the only TS18048/TS2532 sites are the narrow B221 tuple-rest / B279 optional-number-arithmetic / `checkNoUncheckedIndexedCompoundAssign` index-sig walkers — there is NO general property-access-on-maybe-undefined-identifier emitter). So a dedicated session re-applies the proven-safe closure-narrowing change AND adds the general TS18048 emitter together → flips `implicitConstParameters` (the `f5` case needs `x` to stay `string|undefined` inside the closure, which the reassignment gate already handles correctly). RISK of the closure-narrowing piece is now KNOWN-LOW (proven net-zero); the new TS18048 emitter is the MED-risk part (broad strictNullChecks surface). Backpointer: round-183 session note + the `implicitConstParameters` diff (FP TS2339 @ 7:27 + missing TS18048 @ 44:27).

- [ ] **B450-ID-OUTPUT-DECOUPLING — ⚠ DEMOTED to LOW-priority correctness task 2026-06-16 round 175 (was PROMOTED round 174 on a MIS-MEASUREMENT).** Round-174 promoted this as potentially-high-yield on the claim "338/364 failures are order-dependent". **Round-175 RE-MEASURED CORRECTLY (round-174's measurement used a regex XML parser that mis-attributes self-closing passed-testcase tags to the next failure): the 364 failures are GENUINE and DETERMINISTIC — running the 329 failing-base patterns as an isolated batch reproduces all 364. Order-dependence is real but bounded to ~25–50 boundary tests (the −25 magnitude), in BOTH directions, so any global re-ordering is a WASH-or-NEGATIVE unless the structural order EXACTLY matches tsc everywhere. This is a determinism-hygiene task, NOT a yield play — do it for correctness, expect ~0 net test flips.** Original analysis (still accurate as root-cause): `Type.nextTypeId` and `Symbol.nextId` are global monotonic counters whose `resetIdCounter()` is NEVER called, so absolute ids drift across the JVM test run and leak into id-keyed HashMap/HashSet iteration order and id-tie-break sorts → ORDER-DEPENDENT diagnostic output (e.g. `numericClassMembers1`/`noSymbolForMergeCrash` pass in isolation but flip in the full suite; the CLAUDE.md "Test ordering sensitivity" gotcha is this). **DO NOT just reset the ids per-compilation — ATTEMPTED round 174 (lazy `resetForCompilation()` at `compileParsed` top, reset to post-class-load base above the 18 intrinsic singletons), REVERTED at net −25: freezing ids makes full-suite==isolation, which breaks MORE tests (those that only pass under full-suite ordering) than it fixes.** The CORRECT direction is to REMOVE id-dependence from the diagnostic-producing paths: audit every `HashMap<Int,…>`/`HashSet<Int>` (id-keyed) whose ITERATION ORDER reaches output, and every `.sortedBy { it.id }` / id tie-break, and replace the id key/tie-break with a STABLE structural key (source position, then name). Prime suspects to grep: `getUnionType` member sort (sorts by `it.id`), `aliasDisplayMap`/`unionAliasStructural` lookups, any `mutableSetOf<Int>()` side-channel iterated for display, "and N more" property-set ordering, `relationComparisonStack`/`isDeeplyNested` (id-occurrence — likely order-stable already). RISK: MED-HIGH but the diagnostic is a full-suite failing-set diff after EACH narrowed change (start with `getUnionType` sort → LinkedHashSet/structural sort). YIELD: ~0 net test flips expected (the −25 experiment proves ~25 tests are order-sensitive, but in BOTH directions — stabilizing them to a structural order that doesn't perfectly match tsc trades passes for failures). NOTE: `getUnionType` already sorts by `flags.value` (stable, NOT id) and the visible `.sortedBy { it.id }` sites (missingMembers, union members at Checker.kt ~93857/93873) preserve RELATIVE id order = creation order = source order (offset-invariant under reset), so the −25 likely comes from `HashSet<Int>`/`HashMap<Int>` ITERATION order (bucket = absolute-id % capacity) reaching output, NOT from the sorts — audit which side-channel id-sets are iterated for display. Backpointer: round-175 correction + round-174 session note + STATUS.md.

### STRATEGIC MAP of the remaining failures (2026-05-30, pipeline-optimization session)

**The "surgical pool exhausted → only architectural blockers left" framing that
drove ~20 rounds of recon-only / +1 sessions was a TOOL ARTIFACT, not reality.**
`find_candidates.py` only ever scanned tests that already emit a parseable
`error TSxxxx` line (the EXTRA/MISSING/SWAP buckets) — that slice is **33 of the
1014 current failures**. The tool was blind to the other ~70%. It has now been
extended (commit `tooling(queue): un-blind …`) with two new buckets. Full map of
the 1014 failures (from the 2026-05-30 fresh XML set):

| bucket | count | what it means | tool flag |
|---|---|---|---|
| **NONE-PRODUCED** | **611** (590 fresh) | baseline expects errors, we emit nothing | `--none` |
| error-baseline diff (EXTRA/MISSING/SWAP) | 307 (33 in ≤3-code window) | we emit something, wrong/incomplete | default |
| **OUTPUT** (JS/decl/sourcemap) | 105 (≈95% already skip-logged) | pure output diff, no codes | `--output` |

**NONE-PRODUCED is the path to 100%.** It is dominated by the hard type-engine
families (TS2322 ×91, TS2345 ×38, TS2339 ×37 — these need the assignability /
generic-inference / cross-file-scope blockers), BUT it has a large **BOUNDED
tail that is genuinely surgical and was never triaged**:

- **TS2307 ×14 (module resolution)** — e.g. `es6ExportAll` does `import … from 'server'`
  where `server.ts` exists but the specifier is BARE (non-relative, not a
  node_modules package); TS fires TS2307, we wrongly resolve it. Shared root
  cause across most of the 14 → likely one fix flips several. **Best first target.**
- **TS2403 ×9 (subsequent var decls must have same type)** — `promiseIdentity*`
  cluster (5) shares a Promise-interface-merge root; more type-engine-coupled.
- **TS2367 ×9 (comparison appears unintentional)**, **TS2688 ×5 (cannot find type-def file)**,
  **TS2540 ×5 (readonly assignment)**, **TS2554 ×4 (arg count)**, **TS2344 ×4**,
  **TS4023/TS4025 (decl emit)**, **TS2305/TS2649/TS2551/TS2552 …** — each a
  bounded check, 2–3 expected error lines, mostly fresh.

**Process rule going forward:** run `find_candidates.py --none --fresh` (and
`--code TSxxxx` to focus) BEFORE ever declaring the pool dry. A session is only
"pool exhausted" when EXTRA/MISSING/SWAP **and** the bounded NONE tail **and**
OUTPUT are all dry — which is not the case today.

### Known architectural blockers (RESHUFFLED 2026-04-25, 8337 passing)

These blockers recur across multiple "close-to-passing" tests and cannot be fixed with surgical changes in a single session. Any agent attempting these should plan for a multi-session investigation with regression budget.

**Priority ordering** — ranked by (expected tests unblocked) ÷ (regression risk × refactor scope). Higher rank = better cost/benefit. Reshuffled after the 16.4gq–gs session: the surgical pool has been ground down to zero across 5+ sessions, so the path forward is committing entire sessions to architectural work. Per the original Phase 16 impact analysis (line ~2479), the largest unaddressed feature is **control flow narrowing** (~250 tests potential, only partial done in 16.3) — it now sits at #1.

**What changed in this reshuffle:**
- **NEW #1**: Full control flow narrowing (was 16.3 PARTIAL — promoted to architectural blocker since the remaining work is full flow-graph construction, not surgical).
- **NEW #2**: Generic argument inference (was implicit under "16.4 generic instantiation" — promoted because tests like `genericConstraintSatisfaction1`, `superCallArgsMustMatch`, `genericClassWithStaticFactory`, `superWithTypeArgument3`, `privacyCheckAnonymousFunctionParameter2` all hit the same gap: we don't infer `T` from arg type then check the constraint).
- **#3 (was #5)**: Cross-file global scope conflation — bumped UP. Each session that does skip-log auditing finds more tests gated on this; estimated impact revised upward.
- **#4 (was #1)**: Structural comparison of generic refs — DEMOTED to LOW yield. Steps (a)–(d) infrastructure landed; the remaining gaps are bounded (different-target TS2352, overload-aware TS2416 for lib types, multi-statement return-from-body inference) and total ≤ 10 tests across all sub-cases.
- **#5–#7**: JSDoc, TS7006 over-suppression, Parser error-recovery — unchanged ordering, all LOW yield.
- **NEW MAINT-1**: Stale skip-log audit (16.4gp/gr/gs sessions all found stale entries — running this once could surface +5–15 candidates that are already passing).

#### 1. Full control flow narrowing — HIGHEST yield (~100+ tests realistic), MEDIUM risk

**Status (2026-04-25):** 16.3 landed surgical narrowing fixes (TS1344 message, TS2739/TS2740 multi-missing, null/undefined narrowing in `if (x == null)` branches, narrowing helper for variable declarations). Full flow-graph construction was deferred. The remaining gap is the dominant blocker for TS2339 (549 tests with this code), TS2454 (103 deficit), TS2774 (118 tests).

**Failing test patterns that need this:**
- ~~`nestedLoopTypeGuards_ts`~~ flipped 17.30a (flow-graph TS2454 body-recursion)
- ~~`typeGuardConstructorDerivedClass_ts`~~ flipped 17.5a (`x.constructor === Class` narrowing)
- ~~`narrowingUnionToNeverAssigment_ts`~~ flipped 17.1b (var-decl `never` target narrowing)
- ~~`narrowByEquality_ts`~~ (TS2839 narrowing — partially done in 16.4fy but limited)
- ~~`optionalChainWithInstantiationExpression1_ts`~~ flipped 17.44 (synthetic-paren marker for instantiation-expr-breaks-chain pattern)
- Many TS2774 "always defined" / TS2454 "used before assigned" with non-trivial control flow.

- **Yield**: 60–100 tests realistic (250 upper bound from impact analysis × ~30% realistic factor).
- **Scope**: build a real flow graph during binding (per-function basic blocks + branch edges), reify narrowed types per AST position. tsgo's `internal/checker/flow.go` (2.7K lines) is the reference implementation. This is INFRASTRUCTURE work — expect 2–4 sessions to land safely.
- **Risk**: MEDIUM. Narrowing is opt-in (a position with no narrow info falls back to declared type), so regressions are bounded to over-narrowing (false NEVER) rather than wholesale changes.
- **Why first now**: highest absolute yield. The original Phase 16 ordering put 16.3 fourth ("orthogonal but complex; defer to after easier wins") but the easier wins are now exhausted — narrowing IS the next big unlock.

#### 2. Generic argument inference (NEW) — HIGH yield (~20–40 tests), MEDIUM risk

When calling a generic function with concrete args, TypeScript infers each type parameter from the corresponding argument type, then checks each inferred T against its constraint. Currently we punt on this entirely — generic call sites with type-param-typed args either pass trivially (T = anyType) or skip the assignability check.

**Failing test patterns that need this:**
- ~~`genericConstraintSatisfaction1_ts`~~: `f<T extends S>(x: T)` called with `{s: 1}` against `S = {s: string}` — should emit TS2322 + TS6500.
- ~~`genericClassWithStaticFactory_ts`~~: `T` not inferred from arg → no TS2345.
- ~~`superCallArgsMustMatch_ts`~~, ~~`superWithTypeArgument3_ts`~~: `super(...)` arg checking when base is generic.
- ~~`privacyCheckAnonymousFunctionParameter2_ts`~~: function-typed parameter not structurally compared.
- ~~`widenToAny1_ts`~~, ~~`widenToAny2_ts`~~: `string | undefined` initializer widened wrong against `number` annotation.

- **Yield**: 20–40 tests. Each individual test gives +1, but the pattern repeats across many test files.
- **Scope**: extend `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` and `checkArgumentsAgainstOverloads` to: (a) collect inferences from arg→param matching (each TypeParam → concrete type), (b) apply constraint check per TypeParam, (c) substitute inferred types into the param type and recheck. Touches the existing overload-resolution and constraint-checking paths.
- **Risk**: MEDIUM. Could regress overload-resolution tests if inference picks wrong overload. Mitigate with conservative gating (only when ALL TypeParams resolve to non-error types).
- **Why second**: stacks naturally on Blocker #4 step (a)/(b)/(c)/(d) infrastructure (Type.Reference interning, `resolveGenericPropertyType` mapper, `getPropertyTypeForRelation` cache) — the building blocks are already there.

#### 3. Cross-file global scope conflation — MEDIUM-HIGH yield (~30+ tests, was MEDIUM), HIGHEST risk

`Checker.init` does `mergeSymbolTable(globals, result.locals)` for every binderResult, merging module-file exports into the shared `globals` map alongside script-file locals and lib. In module files, identifiers from OTHER module files that weren't imported appear in `scope.has(name)` as if they were globals.

Blocks fine-grained module-visibility diagnostics: TS2301 vs TS2663 distinction, default-import-from-export-equals visibility, namespace export filtering across files.

**Failing test patterns:**
- ~~`classMemberInitializerWithLamdaScoping4_ts`~~ (TS2301 vs TS2663)
- `moduleAugmentationsImports3/4_ts` (TS2339 FP on nested module augmentation)
- ~~`errorsOnImportedSymbol_ts`~~ (cross-file type-only flagging)
- ~~`jsFileCompilationLetDeclarationOrder2_ts`~~, ~~`jsFileCompilationDuplicateVariableErrorReported_ts`~~
- Possibly contributes to many `genericSpecializations*`, `arrayAssignmentTest*` cross-namespace cases not yet diagnosed.

- **Yield**: 30+ tests. Each skip-log audit session surfaces more candidates blocked here.
- **Scope**: split "true globals" (script-file locals + lib + KNOWN_GLOBALS) from per-file module locals; reconstruct `fileScope` to only include imports the current file actually declares. Touches **every identifier-resolution check** in the checker (~30 call sites).
- **Risk**: HIGHEST. A naive implementation regresses 50+ tests. Recommended approach: introduce per-file-scope construction behind a flag, run side-by-side, then flip per file as the scope construction proves correct.
- **Why third (bumped from last)**: yield estimate revised upward after multiple skip-log audits found this blocker named in the residual; without addressing it, ~30 surgical-looking tests remain stuck.

#### 4. Structural comparison of generic type references — LOW yield (was MEDIUM), MEDIUM risk

**Status (2026-04-25, 8337 passing):** Steps (a), (b), (c) partial, (d) partial all landed across 16.4dc–df, dg, dm, gr, gs. Type.Reference interning, cycle/depth heuristic, TypeParam apparent-type comparison, `resolvedPropertyTypes` cache, heritage-clause type-arg instantiation, base-chain inheritance via `resolveGenericPropertyType`, parameter-mismatch chain elaboration, base-method TypeParam TS2208 — all in place.

**What's left (small, gated by other blockers):**
- ~~Different-target TS2352 with "Property X missing" elaboration (`genericTypeAssertions2_ts`)~~ — flipped 17.28 (narrow array-source → class-target case). Generalizing to all different-target casts still requires a TypeScript-style `comparable` relation with full structural elaboration.
- Overload-aware TS2416 for lib generic interfaces (`implementArrayInterface_ts` emits 2 extra diagnostics because lib's `every`/`filter` have type-predicate overloads that user code only implements one of).
- ~~Multi-statement bodies with single return for `inferSimpleReturnTypeFromBody`.~~ **Closed B4.1 (2026-05-13, +0 foundation)** — helper at Checker.kt:39694 now handles multi-statement bodies via top-level ReturnStatement collection.
- ~~Identifier returns (`return param`, `return this.field`) for return-type-from-body inference.~~ **Closed B4.2 + B4.3 (2026-05-13, +0 foundation)** — `is Identifier` arm extended to `return paramName` for annotated params; new `is PropertyAccessExpression` arm handles `return this.field` for class-property-typed fields (new `enclosingClass: Type.Interface?` parameter threaded from `resolveGenericPropertyType` caller at Checker.kt:39625).

- **Yield**: ≤ 10 tests across all sub-cases.
- **Scope**: contained to checker (no binder/parser changes).
- **Why demoted**: infrastructure work is largely done; remaining test gains are individual-test scale, not pattern-level. Best handled opportunistically when a specific case aligns with surgical session's budget.

#### 5. JSDoc type annotations — LOW yield (~5–10 tests), LOW risk

`@type {T}`, `@this {T}`, `@typedef`, `@param {T}` — we bind `@typedef` nominally but don't honor `@type` assertions or `@this` parameter types.

- **Yield**: 5–10 `.js` file tests.
- **Scope**: JSDoc parsing (scanner comment handling) and a new JSDoc-to-TypeNode bridge in the checker. Does not touch non-JS checking paths.
- **Why fifth (was second)**: low blast radius means low risk, but absolute yield is small. Higher-yield blockers (#1, #2, #3) take priority for "session-scale architectural work" budget.

#### 6. TS7006 over-suppression for callback parameters — LOW yield (~3–5 tests), MEDIUM risk

We suppress TS7006 for any `contextuallyTyped=true` param. TypeScript only suppresses when the contextual type actually provides param types (bidirectional inference succeeds).

- **Yield**: 3–5 tests (`intraBindingPattern`, `subtypeReduction`, `signatureCombiningRestParameters`).
- **Scope**: requires bidirectional inference plumbing in contextual typing.
- **Why sixth**: low yield + needs new infrastructure. Defer.

#### 7. Parser error-recovery asymmetry — LOW yield (1–2 per case), HIGH risk per attempt

Several tests expect specific token-consumption-then-continue recovery (e.g. `declare class foo();` → class with empty body + phantom `()` statement + next function).

- **Yield**: 1–2 tests per case, case-by-case.
- **Scope**: per-case parser edits.
- **Why last**: per-case work, no multiplier, regression-prone. Best handled opportunistically.

#### MAINT-1. Stale skip-log audit — UNKNOWN yield (likely 5–15 tests), LOW risk

Every recent session that audited the skip log found 1–3 entries marked "stuck on Blocker #X" that have actually been passing for sessions (e.g. 16.4gl-recon found `genericCloneReturnTypes`/`genericCloneReturnTypes2` stale; 16.4go found `generics4`/`genericDerivedTypeWithSpecializedBase`/`genericPrototypeProperty3` stale; this session found `genericPrototypeProperty3` again). The cumulative impact of stale entries blocking the candidate finder is probably 5–15 tests.

- **Yield**: unknown but likely 5–15 candidates that re-surface as fresh.
- **Scope**: walk every entry in the "Explored-but-skipped tests" section, run the test, mark stale (~), update reason if still failing.
- **Risk**: zero (read-only investigation).
- **Why a maintenance task**: not architectural but high-leverage. Should be run as a one-off "audit session" before any session that uses `find_candidates.py --fresh` for surgical work, or after every major architectural fix lands.

---

**Parallelism note**: Blockers #1, #2, #5, #7 can be worked independently. #3 should run after #1/#2 have resolved their fraction of overlapping cases (clearer "remaining failures pointing to #3" set). #4's remaining sub-cases pair naturally with surgical sessions — not a full-session investment. #6 depends on enough contextual-typing infrastructure that #2's inference work should land first.

**Recommended next sessions (in order):**
1. **MAINT-1** (1 session): clean up stale skip log → frees `find_candidates.py --fresh` to find genuine candidates.
2. **Blocker #1 step 1** (1–2 sessions): build flow-graph infra in binder; no behavior change yet.
3. **Blocker #1 step 2** (2 sessions): wire flow-graph into TS2454/TS2339/TS2774 emission sites.
4. **Blocker #2** (2 sessions): generic argument inference + constraint check.
5. **Blocker #3** (3+ sessions): per-file scope construction behind a flag, then per-file flip.

### Candidate-picking workflow for surgical fixes

When context starts a new session, the fastest path to wins is:

1. **Run full suite once** (4-6 min) to produce `build/test-results/jvmTest/*.xml`.
2. **Python-parse the XMLs** to find tests by diff size and pattern. The key filter is 1-2 extra diagnostic lines (too-aggressive checks) or 1 missing diagnostic at a specific position (simple checks to add). Example snippets live in this file's history — reuse them.
3. **Look for code-swap tests** (expected TS####A at position P vs actual TS####B at same position P) — these are often single diagnostic emissions at a specific code path that just need the code changed or position adjusted.
4. **Skip**: tests needing generic variance, module-visibility, JSDoc type handling, or full type inference — those are the architectural blockers above.
5. **Inspect `typescript-repo/tests/cases/compiler/<name>.ts` + `typescript-repo/tests/baselines/reference/<name>.errors.txt`** for context before implementing. Don't just look at the diff.

### Queue execution strategy

**Original Phase 16 sequence (DONE/IN PROGRESS):** 16.0 → 16.1 → 16.2 → 16.3 → 16.4

**Status:** 16.0/16.1/16.2 DONE, 16.3 PARTIAL (full flow-graph deferred → now Blocker #1), 16.4 IN PROGRESS (generic infrastructure largely done; remaining sub-cases moved to Blocker #4).

**NEW sequence (2026-04-25 reshuffle) — as the surgical pool is exhausted, commit entire sessions to architectural blockers:**

**Phase 17 sequence:** MAINT-1 → Blocker #1 (control flow narrowing) → Blocker #2 (generic argument inference) → Blocker #3 (cross-file scope) → Blocker #4 / #5 / #6 / #7 opportunistically.

**Rationale (priority-by-yield × tractability):**
1. **MAINT-1 first** — stale skip-log audit. Read-only, ~1 session, surfaces 5–15 already-passing tests as fresh candidates that would otherwise stay hidden behind `[SKIP]` markers in `find_candidates.py`. Cheap, no regression risk, and clears the deck for #1's diagnosis.
2. **Blocker #1 (control flow narrowing) second** — highest absolute yield (~60–100 tests realistic). The remaining failing tests across many categories (TS2339, TS2454, TS2774, TS2839) all converge on this. Build flow-graph in binder, wire into emitters in two stages.
3. **Blocker #2 (generic argument inference) third** — stacks on the 16.4 generic infrastructure already in place. ~20–40 tests. Lower risk than Blocker #3.
4. **Blocker #3 (cross-file scope) fourth** — high yield but highest risk; defer until #1/#2 have cleared their fraction so the residual is well-characterized.
5. **Blocker #4 (remaining structural generic gaps) opportunistically** — small (~10 tests), pairs with surgical sessions, not full-session work.
6. **Blockers #5/#6/#7** — low yield, defer.

**Hard rules:**
- Each feature must land behind a feature flag or with conservative guards initially.
- Run full suite after EACH sub-step to catch regressions early.
- If a feature causes >20 regressions, add more guards or narrow scope; don't revert without analysis.
- Document each partial implementation in CLAUDE.md gotchas.

**Per-session throughput — fix multiple items per session.** A single sub-step (e.g. 16.4h, 16.4i) is the unit of commit, not the unit of session. After committing + pushing one sub-step, loop back to pick up the next unchecked sub-step. Keep going until (a) the queue is empty, (b) the next item is genuinely blocked, or (c) context/time budget is nearly exhausted — then wrap up with a summary. Two-to-four sub-steps per session is a reasonable target when the items are small (+1 to +5 tests each); larger infrastructure items may consume a full session on their own. Always finish each sub-step cleanly (test suite green, commit + push) before starting the next — never bundle unrelated work into one commit.

**Realistic total gain:** 500-800 new passing tests → ~85-88% pass rate.
